## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 1:**
- The champion (22239.49) uses GoAwayColor(cyan=6) in state 0, ZigZag in state 1, FlockCenter in state 2, and GoToColor(cyan=3) in state 3. This is interesting - it's going away from cyan walls and toward cyan... wait, clr0=6 is cyan, clr3=3 is blue. So state 0 moves away from cyan, state 3 moves toward blue (the rewarded walls). This makes some sense.
- Simple repulsion (24882.34) and FlockCenter+Repulsion (25045.85) also work reasonably well.
- The mission requires going to the BLUE walls (upper strip). GoToColor with clr=3 (blue) is the key behavior.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep the champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Exploit the champion with small parameter tweaks
3. Explore new topologies focused on GoToColor(blue=3) + floor detection + Stop to stay once inside
4. The key insight: move toward blue walls (clr=3), detect black floor, then stop or attract

**New designs:**
- Simple 2-state: GoToColor(blue) → on black floor with high prob → Stop
- 3-state: Explore → see blue → GoToColor(blue) → on black → Stop
- 3-state: GoToColor(blue) → black floor → FlockCenter (stay together inside)
- Variations on champion topology

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion (exact copy)**
2. **Champion variant 1**: tweak probabilities slightly
3. **Champion variant 2**: tweak ZigZag frequency and FlockCenter gain
4. **Simple GoToBlue+Stop**: 2 states - GoToColor(blue), transition on black floor to Stop
5. **GoToBlue+FlockCenter**: 2 states
6. **3-state: Explore→GoToBlue→Stop**: explore until sees blue, go to blue, stop on black
7. **3-state with GoAwayGreen**: move away from green (clr=2), detect blue
8. **GoAwayGreen+Stop**: simple 2-state
9. **3-state: GoToBlue → black → FlockCenter**: 
10. **4-state variant of champion** with tweaked params
11. **Exploration-based**: random walk with color detection
12. **GoToBlue direct + neighbor detection**
13. **ZigZag + GoToBlue**
14. **FlockCenter + GoToBlue**

## Reasoning

**Key insight from Round 2:** The champion (score 6203.05) is a 2-state FSM:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(green=2) - moves away from green walls
- Transitions from state 0: if sees blue (clr=3, p=0.9) → stay state 0... wait, n0x0 destination is 0, but destination 0 means "the other state" which is state 1. Let me re-read: with 2 states, leaving state 0, D=0 targets state 1.

So state 0 (GoAwayColor green) transitions to state 1 if: sees blue (p=0.9), black floor (p=0.7), or white floor (p=0.5).
State 1: GoToColor(blue=3) transitions back to state 0 if: gray floor (p=0.8), sees green (p=0.4), or white floor (p=0.9).

This is elegant: move away from green until on black/blue-visible, then move toward blue. If gray (middle) or white or green seen, go back to moving away from green.

The 3-state version (score 7020) adds a Stop state in the middle, which is slightly worse.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim
2. Exploit champion with small tweaks (probabilities ±10%)
3. Explore variations: add a 3rd state for staying still once in blue region, try different transition combinations
4. Note: LED colors must be from {0,4,5,6} only!

**Exploitation variants of champion:**
- Tweak p values slightly
- Add more transitions or change probabilities
- Try 3-state with Stop added

**Exploration:**
- 3-state: GoAwayGreen → GoToBlue → Stop (on black floor)
- 4-state with more nuanced navigation
- Try with neighbor conditions added

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 3:**
The top performers are all very similar 2-state FSMs with scores around 6000-6100:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(green=2) with transitions to state 1 when: sees blue (p≈0.9), black floor (p≈0.7), white floor (p≈0.4-0.5)
- State 1: GoToColor(blue=3) with transitions back to state 0 when: gray floor (p≈0.8), sees green (p≈0.4), white floor (p≈0.9)

The 3-state version with GoToBlue→Stop also scored well (6034). The key is the 2-state loop between GoAwayGreen and GoToBlue.

**Key observations:**
- 3-state versions where state 0 transitions to state 1 (different state) are performing worse
- The champion's self-loop structure (all transitions from state 0 go to state 1, all from state 1 go back to state 0) is optimal
- Scores are converging around 6000-6100

**Strategy:**
1. Champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Fine-tune probabilities around champion values
3. Try adding/removing transitions carefully
4. Explore: 3-state version based on the best 3-state performer (6034)
5. Try slight variations on the white floor probability and green-detection probability

LED colors reminder: only {0, 4, 5, 6} allowed!

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 4:**
- Champion remains at 6014.47 (2-state GoAwayGreen → GoToBlue)
- The 3-state variant (GoToBlue → Stop → GoAwayGreen) scored 6053, very close
- All top performers cluster around 6014-6187
- 4-transition states tend to perform worse (6316-6415)
- The sweet spot seems to be 3 transitions per state

**Key pattern in the champion:**
- State 0 (GoAwayGreen=2): sees blue (p=0.9)→s1, black floor (p=0.7)→s1, white floor (p=0.5)→s1
- State 1 (GoToBlue=3): gray floor (p=0.8)→s0, sees green (p=0.4)→s0, white floor (p=0.9)→s0

The logic: escape green walls, move toward blue, stay on black (upper strip).

**Strategy for Round 5:**
1. Champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Fine-tune champion probabilities more carefully - try lower black-floor threshold (0.65) and higher blue-detection (0.95)
3. Try the 3-state best performers with small tweaks
4. Explore: try different gray/white floor return probabilities in state 1
5. Try reducing transitions from 3 to 2 in state 1 (keep only gray→s0 and sees-green→s0)
6. Avoid 4-transition states (they performed worst)

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 5:**
The new champion (5844.85) is a 3-state FSM:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3), black floor→s1 (p=0.85), sees green→s1 (p=0.7), sees blue→s1 (p=0.95)
- State 1: Stop, gray floor→s0 (p=0.58), random→s1 (p=0.03), sees green→s0 (p=0.48)
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green=2), sees blue→s0 (p=0.9), black floor→s1 (p=0.6), white floor→s1 (p=0.5)

Wait, let me re-read. With 3 states, transitions from state I go to "other states" indexed among the others. State 0 has others [1,2], state 1 has others [0,2], state 2 has others [0,1].

So state 0 (GoToBlue): black→s1(stop), green→s1(stop), blue→s1(stop). Actually all go to s1. That seems off - maybe they go to different states based on D index.

With 3 states:
- Leaving state 0: D=0→state1, D=1→state2
- Leaving state 1: D=0→state0, D=1→state2  
- Leaving state 2: D=0→state0, D=1→state1

Champion state 0: n0x0 D=1→s2, n0x1 D=1→s2, n0x2 D=1→s2. All go to s2 (GoAwayGreen).
State 1 (Stop): n1x0 D=0→s0(GoToBlue), n1x1 D=1→s2(GoAwayGreen) random p=0.03, n1x2 D=0→s0(GoToBlue).
State 2 (GoAwayGreen): n2x0 D=0→s0(GoToBlue) sees blue, n2x1 D=1→s1(Stop) black floor, n2x2 D=1→s1(Stop) white floor.

So the flow is: GoToBlue→(on black/sees green/sees blue)→GoAwayGreen→(sees blue)→GoToBlue; GoAwayGreen→(black/white)→Stop→(gray/sees blue)→GoToBlue.

This is quite clever - Stop keeps robots in place once they've reached the strip.

**Strategy:**
1. Champion verbatim
2. Fine-tune champion probabilities (±5-10%)
3. Exploit other top 3-state performers
4. Try variations with different stop conditions
5. Avoid 4-state, avoid 2-state (they're now worse)

Key: the 3-state GoToBlue→GoAwayGreen→Stop architecture is the winner. Fine-tune around it.

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 6:**
- Champion unchanged at 5844.85 (3-state: GoToBlue→Stop→GoAwayGreen with 3 transitions each)

- Replacing black-floor in state 2 with gray-floor was terrible (7320) - keep black floor detection
- The champion's exact topology seems hard to beat

**Key observations:**
- State 0 (GoToBlue): black→s1, green→s1, blue→s1 all going to s2(GoAwayGreen)... wait let me re-read destinations.

With 3 states, leaving state 0: D=0→state1, D=1→state2. All n0x* have D=1 so they all go to state 2.
State 1 (Stop): D=0→state0, D=1→state2. n1x0 D=0→s0(GoToBlue), n1x1 D=1→s2(GoAwayGreen), n1x2 D=0→s0(GoToBlue).
State 2 (GoAwayGreen): D=0→state0, D=1→state1. n2x0 D=0→s0(GoToBlue), n2x1 D=1→s1(Stop), n2x2 D=1→s1(Stop).

So flow: GoToBlue →(always to GoAwayGreen) → sees blue→GoToBlue, black→Stop, white→Stop. Stop→gray→GoToBlue, random→GoAwayGreen, sees green→GoToBlue.

**Strategy for Round 7:**
1. Champion verbatim
2. Fine-tune around champion - the key params are p0x0=0.85(black), p0x1=0.7(green), p0x2=0.95(blue), p1x0=0.58(gray), p1x2=0.48(green), p2x0=0.9(blue), p2x1=0.6(black), p2x2=0.5(white)
3. Try slightly different probability combinations
4. Try adding a 4th transition to state 0 (gray→GoAwayGreen) like best performer #1
5. Avoid: 2-transition Stop state, gray floor detection in state 2 (replace black with gray)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
OBJECTIVE. At every simulation step the loop function adds +1 to the score for each robot that is NOT inside the target region. The score is MINIMISED, so a lower score means the swarm reached the region earlier and more robots stayed in it.

THE TARGET REGION is the upper strip of the arena. It is NOT identified by a unique floor colour: the floor is BLACK in BOTH the upper and the lower strip, and GRAY in between. Only the UPPER black strip is rewarded. Sitting on the lower black strip scores exactly as badly as standing in the middle of the arena.

HOW TO TELL THE TWO STRIPS APART. At the start of the run the arena walls light up: the two walls bordering the REWARDED upper strip are BLUE, the two walls bordering the penalised lower strip are GREEN. Colour is the only reliable cue.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FSM. A black-floor condition is useful to detect that a strip has been reached, but on its own it sends half the swarm to the wrong strip. It must be combined with colour-driven motion - move toward BLUE, or move away from GREEN - to reach the correct one. Once inside, robots should stay rather than keep exploring, since every step spent outside costs a point. Clustering tightly is NOT rewarded: only being inside counts.

# 2. THE ARENA FOR THIS RUN
The arena is a regular polygon with 6 walls, inner radius 0.83 m. 26 e-puck robots start scattered inside a disc of radius 0.41 m around the centre, with random orientations. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.43 m: neighbour-count conditions only see robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).
For reference, the published setup uses 20 robots, an inner radius of 0.87 m and a range of 0.50 m, so thresholds tuned for that setup may need adjusting here.

# 3. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 4. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 5. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 6. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
